Um... yeah, I think so. I mean, even when I've been at a weight that my parents or my doctor said was totally fine and healthy, I still felt that same panic about gaining any more.

Um... I guess... I'm not really sure, honestly. I don't think it's always because I'm super hungry, but... I don't know if I'd say it's just because I'm stressed out, either.

Um... I feel like I'm frustrating you, and I really don't mean to. I know you're trying to figure this out, and I'm just... I'm really sorry if I'm not making sense.

Like something else takes over. And even while I'm sitting there in my head telling myself to stop, I just keep going until I absolutely can't stand it anymore. I'm really trying to be better about it, though. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... that's kind of hard to admit. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything, but... yeah. I do. \n\nIt's just, after I eat like that, I get this overwhelming panic. I feel absolutely terrified that all the food is just sitting there, turning into weight, and I just can't handle the feeling. So I usually end up sneaking off to a bathroom in the dorm, or sometimes I'll find a really quiet floor of the library... and I'll make myself throw up. \n\nI know it's a really bad thing to do, and I promise I'm trying to stop. I hope you're not too disappointed in me for it. Is that... I mean, does that make sense why I'd react that way?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating until you can't stand it anymore happen?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, that’s... I really don't want to sound like a lost cause or anything, and I promise I'm trying to do better. But... I guess for most of this semester, it’s been happening... well, several times a week. Not every single day! Just... you know, several times. \n\nBut if I'm being completely honest with you, looking back over at least the last few months... there really hasn’t been a week where the whole cycle just stopped. I know that probably sounds really bad, and I'm so sorry I let it get this out of hand. I hope you don't think less of me for it being this frequent... is it okay that I'm telling you all this?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard to keep things balanced, so my weight hasn't really changed much at all. I think I look pretty much the same as I did when I started the semester. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm, like, letting myself go or anything like that. I actually try to be really careful about it. So, no, no big changes. Does that... I mean, is that a good thing? I hope that's what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Yeah, I am. I mean, I know guys aren't really supposed to care that much about this stuff, and I don't want to sound vain or obsessed or anything... but it really messes with my head. \n\nLike, I could be having a totally decent day in classes, doing fine, but... if I'm walking back and I catch my reflection in a dorm window, and I think my stomach or my face looks bigger than the other guys around me... it just ruins everything. It feels like I've just failed at everything, and I suddenly don't want anyone to even see me. \n\nI try not to let it get to me, I promise I do. It just... it hits me really hard sometimes. Is it normal to feel that way? I hope I don't sound too crazy for letting something like a reflection ruin my day.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes of eating like that without making yourself vomit afterwards?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I really try to just be normal about it, I swear I do. But... no, not usually. \n\nI just... when I have one of those episodes, I get this overwhelming, terrifying feeling that all that food is just sitting there, turning straight into weight. It makes me panic so much. So... I usually end up finding a bathroom in the dorm, or maybe going up to a really quiet floor in the library where nobody goes, and... I make myself throw up. \n\nI know it’s gross, and I’m so embarrassed to even admit it. I really, really don't want to be doing it. I know it's not the right way to handle things. I just... I get so scared, you know? I hope you understand... I'm really trying to be honest with you. Is it okay that I told you that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure? I don't... I don't think I'm seeing things that aren't there, if that's what you mean. Like, I know my actual size, and I think my friends and everyone else probably see me the exact same way I see myself. I just... I probably just judge it a lot harsher than they do, you know? \n\nI really hope that's an okay answer. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of touch with reality or anything like that. I try to stay pretty grounded about the facts of what I look like, even if I get upset about it. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you...\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you were at a weight that others considered healthy?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. I mean, even when I've been at a weight that my parents or my doctor said was totally fine and healthy, I still felt that same panic about gaining any more. \n\nIt's not that I want to be dangerously skinny or anything like that, I promise. I just... I always feel like I'm right on the edge, you know? Like if I gain even a little bit, it'll just spiral out of control and I'll look awful. So even at a \"normal\" weight, I'm still really scared of it going up. \n\nI know that probably sounds kind of irrational. I really try to be logical about it, but the fear is just always there. Is that a common thing for guys to feel? I hope I'm not the only one who gets like this...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating like that without making yourself vomit afterwards, but still felt like you lost control over how much you were eating?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so? Like, I told you about how I usually handle it when I mess up and eat too much... I pretty much always make sure I fix it afterwards. \n\nI don't really have times where I just eat a ton and then... do nothing. I guess I'm too scared of what would happen if I didn't take care of it right away. I mean, maybe there was a time or two a long time ago, but... no, not really anymore. \n\nI hope that's the right answer... I'm really trying to give you the information you need. I just don't really let myself get away with doing that without fixing it, you know? Is that... is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control over how much you were eating during one of these episodes, even if you didn't make yourself vomit afterwards?\n\nPatient: Um... I think... wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or, well, maybe I misunderstood the question before. I'm sorry if I wasn't clear enough. \n\nBut, um, no. Like I said, I don't really have times where I eat a lot, feel out of control, and then just... let it sit there. I always make sure I deal with it. I really try not to let things get completely out of hand without, you know, managing it afterwards. \n\nI hope I'm understanding you right this time. I'm really trying to be helpful and give you the right answers. Did I explain that okay, or did you mean something else?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control over how much you were eating during a meal, without later making yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm so sorry, doctor. I feel like I must be doing a really terrible job of explaining myself today since you keep having to ask me this. I really don't mean to be difficult, I promise. \n\nBut... yeah, no, I really don't ever do that. If I lose control and eat too much, I always... you know, take care of it. I couldn't just sit there and not do anything about it after messing up like that. \n\nAm I... am I answering this wrong? Is there a specific thing you're looking for me to say? I'm really sorry if I'm frustrating you, I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these episodes of eating until you can't stand it anymore?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, I guess anyone would feel a little bad after doing that, right? \n\nIt's not... I try not to let it ruin my whole day or anything, but... yeah, I do feel pretty awful about it. Like I let myself down. I just feel really ashamed that I couldn't stop myself. \n\nBut I'm working on it! I really am trying to be better about it. Is it normal to feel that way? I hope it doesn't make me sound too... weak or something. I just want to be honest with you so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're hungry during these episodes, or is it more because of stress, anxiety, or other emotions?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess... I'm not really sure, honestly. I don't think it's always because I'm super hungry, but... I don't know if I'd say it's just because I'm stressed out, either. \n\nI mean, everyone eats a little extra when they're stressed sometimes, right? It's not like I'm doing it *just* because I'm anxious. Sometimes it just... happens. I try not to overthink it too much because I don't want to make a bigger deal out of it than it is. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too vague. I just... I don't always know exactly why it starts, and I don't want to give you the wrong idea. Am I explaining that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating episodes are usually triggered by feelings of stress, anxiety, or other emotions?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm sorry, I feel like I just gave a really confusing answer to this a second ago. It's not like that. Sometimes I might feel a little stressed about school or something, but... it doesn't feel like a direct trigger. I don't really notice a strong connection like that. \n\nI hope that's okay to say. I know sometimes doctors look for a specific root cause, and I feel bad that I can't just point to one for you. Am I being helpful at all? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still have these episodes of eating until you can't stand it anymore if you didn't have a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's... I just try to stay healthy and look okay. \n\nI'm not sure if the eating would completely stop if I didn't care about my weight. Maybe? But I really couldn't say for certain. I definitely don't sit down and think, \"I'm scared of gaining weight, so I'm going to eat all of this.\" That would be kind of backwards, wouldn't it? \n\nI just... I don't really know what drives it. It just sort of happens, and then afterward I feel really guilty and worried about my weight. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you...\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you were at a weight that others considered healthy?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. I mean, even when I've been at a weight that my parents or my doctor said was totally fine and healthy, I still felt that same panic about gaining any more. \n\nIt's not that I want to be dangerously skinny or anything like that, I promise. I mean, I guess anyone would feel a little bad after doing that, right? \n\nIt's not... I try not to let it ruin my whole day or anything, but... yeah, I do feel pretty awful about it. I hope it doesn't make me sound too... weak or something. I just want to be honest with you so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating because you're hungry during these episodes, or is it more because of stress, anxiety, or other emotions?\n\nPatient: Um...
